- `Racers` has an `FCrowdyKitRacer` each: `Player`, `X` and `Y` in world metres, `Lap`, `NextGate` (the index in `Gates` of the gate to pass next), `BestLapMs`, `Place` and `TotalMs` once they finish, and `bDidNotFinish`. A value that is not there yet reads 0.

The events arrive as one message, **RaceEvent** (`FCrowdyKitRaceEvent`), heard as the arena's is: **Go** has `At`; **Timing** has a `Stage` (an `ECrowdyKitRaceStage`: **Started**, **Checkpoint**, **Lap** or **Finished**, with **Has Stage** false for one this client does not know), `Player`, `Checkpoint`, `Lap` and `ElapsedMs`; **Violation** has `Player`, `What` and `Detail`, for a report the hub cut or held back; **Retired** has `Player` and `Why`, `left` or `idle`; and **Finished** has `Results`, an `FCrowdyKitRaceResult` for every racer with their `Player`, `Place` and `TotalMs`, the finishers first in place order and then, with 0 for both, those who did not finish.

Where the SDK's copy differs from the platform's [Races and ball games](/game-kit/modules/racing):

- **Positions are world metres, z up,** for the gates, `Move`, the State, the ghost and the events. The platform's keep x and z in its own units from the course's origin.
- **A NaN or infinite position is refused `bad_request`,** and changes nothing.
- **The movement check is the arena's travel budget,** so a client that reports more often does not move faster, a slow connection is not cut short, and the hub's own placements on the line never count against a racer.
- **No flying start.** The countdown holds racers on the line and the start sets them there with no travel saved, so a racer cannot be past the line at the go.
- **A gate between two reports counts,** and its time is when the path crossed it, not when the report arrived. The platform's checks only the reported points, so a racer who reports less often could miss a gate, and is timed later.
- **A race always ends.** It closes when every racer has finished or retired, `finish_grace_ms` after the winner, or at `time_limit_ms`. A racer who leaves or stops reporting retires. The platform's waits for every racer to finish.
- **Snapshots are not sent for every report:** at most five a second, as the arena's, and one at once when the race starts, goes, or closes.
- **Results list every racer, the finishers first in place order,** and a time reaches the board whatever the length of the race's Instance Id. The ghost covers the whole run, however long.

**In Blueprint**, with the **Crowdy Server Object** component holding `CSO_Kit_Race` and `mill-loop-1`:

1. On the join button, add **Call Join (CSO_Kit_Race)**. The first to join then presses a button wired to **Call Start**.
2. Add **On RaceEvent Received (CSO_Kit_Race)**, bound at **Event BeginPlay** with the component as its **Target**. From **Received**, **Switch on ECrowdyKitRaceEventType**: on **Go**, hide the countdown; on **Timing**, show **Lap** and **Elapsed Ms** when **Player** is the signed-in player; on **Finished**, show the podium from **Results**.
3. Add **On Racers Changed (CSO_Kit_Race)**, and for the signed-in player's racer, light the gate at **Next Gate** in the State's **Gates**.

**In C++**, with `Race` the acquired `CSO_Kit_Race`, the pawn finds the gate to drive to next:

```cpp
bool AMillCart::NextGate(FVector& OutWhere) const
{
	const FCrowdyKitRaceState* State = Race->GetState().GetPtr<FCrowdyKitRaceState>();
	if (!State || State->Phase != ECrowdyKitRacePhase::Racing)
	{
		return false;
	}

	const int64 Me = Race->GetSignedInUserId();
	const FCrowdyKitRacer* Mine = State->Racers.FindByPredicate([Me](const FCrowdyKitRacer& Racer) { return Racer.Player == Me; });
	if (!Mine || Mine->bDidNotFinish || Mine->Place > 0 || !State->Gates.IsValidIndex(Mine->NextGate))
	{
		return false;
	}

	const FCrowdyKitRaceGate& Gate = State->Gates[Mine->NextGate];
	OutWhere = FVector(Gate.X * 100.0, Gate.Y * 100.0, GetActorLocation().Z);
	return true;
}
```

### Play a ball game

On the village green, two teams play ball in `green-1`. Add `ball`, deploy, and have each player's pawn hold `CSO_Kit_Ball` with that Instance Id in a **Crowdy Server Object** component.

The pitch is a rectangle of the world. In `CSO_Kit_Ball`'s **Game Kit** category, **Config** places it: `origin` is its corner in world metres, `width` runs along X from there (60 by default) and `depth` along Y (40), and each end wall, at `origin` and at `origin` plus `width`, has a goal `goal_width` wide (12) at its middle. Red attacks the far end, at `origin` plus `width`, and blue the near end. To draw the ball, set `realtime` to `true` and give **Kit Actors**' kind `ball` your ball's class, as [Show a starter's actors](#show-a-starters-actors) says: the ball is then shown to every player within reach of the pitch, at `height` metres (0 by default). With `realtime` on, the match's Instance Id may be at most 29 characters, with no `@` or spaces, since the ball's actor carries it. The other keys, each with its default:

- `team_max` (5), `score_to_win` (3) and `duration_ms` (300000, five minutes).
- `claim_radius` (1.5 metres), how near the ball a player must be to claim it, and `protect_ms` (1500), how long a new holder cannot be robbed.
- `pass_speed` (18) and `shoot_speed` (28) in metres a second, and `friction` (0.8), the share of its speed a loose ball loses each second.
- `max_speed` (9), `teleport` (15) and `grace` (1.25), the movement check as the race's.
- `idle_ms` (15000), how long a player may go without a report while the game is played before they are taken off the pitch; 0 never does.

The functions:

- **Join** takes a **Team** (`FCrowdyKitBallJoinInputs`), **Red** or **Blue**, and puts the player at the middle of their half; a full team is refused `full`. Before the game and after it, Join again changes team. While it is played, a player keeps their team (another is refused `started`), and one who left comes back where they left, so leaving and joining is not a shortcut across the pitch.
- **Start** is called by the first to join, or a developer, and needs a player on each team (`not_ready`). It puts every player at the middle of their half and the ball at the centre.
- **Claim** takes a loose ball within `claim_radius`, or the ball from an opponent once their `protect_ms` is over. It is refused `out_of_reach`, `conflict` when a teammate has it, and `cooldown` while the holder is protected or when claiming twice within half a second.
- **Pass** takes **To** (`FCrowdyKitBallPassInputs`), a teammate's user id (`bad_request` for anyone else), and **Shoot** an aim **X** and **Y** in world metres; both are refused `conflict` when the player does not have the ball. The ball runs on, slows, and turns off the side and end walls; it scores when it crosses an end wall inside the goal.
- **Leave** takes the player off the pitch; a ball they held drops loose where they were. **Move** is the position function. **GetState** answers the State.

The State, which everyone sees:

- `Phase` reads **Lobby**, **Playing** or **Finished**; `Origin`, `Width`, `Depth` and `GoalWidth` are the Config's.
- `Ball` is an `FCrowdyKitBall`: `X` and `Y` in world metres, `VX` and `VY` in metres a second, `Holder` (0 when loose), `HeldSince` and `LastTouch`. A held ball is where its holder is.
- `Players` has an `FCrowdyKitBallPlayer` each: `Player`, `Team`, `X` and `Y`. `Score` is red's then blue's. `EndsAt` is when time runs out, 0 outside a game being played, and `Winner` the winning team when **Has Winner** is true (false before the end, and for a draw).

The events arrive as one message, **BallEvent** (`FCrowdyKitBallEvent`, whose `Type` is an `ECrowdyKitBallEventType`): **Claimed** (`Player`, `Team`, and `From`, the user id they took it from, 0 for a loose ball), **Passed** (`Player`, `To`, `Team`), **Shot** (`Player`, `Team`), **Goal** (`Team`, `Player`, the last to touch it, and `Score`), **Violation** (`Player`, `What`, `Detail`), **Left** (`Player`, `Team`), **Removed** (`Player`, `Team`, and `Why`, `idle`) and **Finished** (`Winner` with **Has Winner**, and `Score`).

Where the SDK's copy differs from the platform's [Races and ball games](/game-kit/modules/racing):

- **Positions are world metres, z up,** and `origin` places the pitch. The platform's keep the pitch at its own origin, and show the ball at an `anchor` chunk.
- **The ball's actor names its match.** Its **Instance Id** on the Crowdy Kit Actor component is the match's, so a client with several games near each other knows which ball is whose; it reaches every player within the pitch's size of it.
- **A NaN or infinite position or aim is refused `bad_request`,** and changes nothing.
- **There is a Leave, and a player may join a game being played,** at their place when they come back. The platform's has no way to leave and refuses a join once the game starts, so a player who dropped could never return.
- **A player who stops reporting is taken off the pitch** after `idle_ms`, with a **Removed** event, and the ball they held drops loose. The platform's keeps them, and the ball, until the game ends.
- **The movement check is the arena's travel budget,** the pitch's edge is no strike, and **Start** puts the teams at their halves.

**In Blueprint**, with the component holding `CSO_Kit_Ball` and `green-1`:

1. On the team buttons, add **Call Join (CSO_Kit_Ball)** with **Team** set to **Red** or **Blue**.
2. On the kick button, add **Call Claim** when **Holder** in the State's **Ball** is not the signed-in player, and **Call Shoot** with the goal's X and Y otherwise: red's goal is at **Origin X** plus **Width**, and both goals at **Origin Y** plus **Depth** over 2.
3. Add **On BallEvent Received (CSO_Kit_Ball)**, bound at **Event BeginPlay**, and on **Goal** show **Score**.

**In C++**, with `Ball` the acquired `CSO_Kit_Ball`, the pawn shoots at the goal it attacks:

```cpp
void AGreenPlayer::ShootAtGoal(ECrowdyKitBallTeam MyTeam)
{
	const FCrowdyKitBallState* State = Ball->GetState().GetPtr<FCrowdyKitBallState>();
	if (!State || State->Phase != ECrowdyKitBallPhase::Playing || State->Ball.Holder != Ball->GetSignedInUserId())
	{
		return;
	}

	FCrowdyKitGroundPosition Aim;
	Aim.X = MyTeam == ECrowdyKitBallTeam::Red ? State->Origin.X + State->Width : State->Origin.X;
	Aim.Y = State->Origin.Y + State->Depth / 2.0;
	Ball->Call(TEXT("Shoot"), FInstancedStruct::Make(Aim), [](const FCrowdyServerCallResult& Result)
	{
		UE_CLOG(!Result.IsSuccess(), LogTemp, Log, TEXT("No shot: %s (%s)"), *Result.Code.ToString(), *Result.Reason);
	});
}
```

The ball's actor is drawn and moved by the SDK; its **Crowdy Kit Actor** component's **Owner** is the holder, so a glow on the held ball listens to **On Kit Actor Changed**. The platform's [Races and ball games](/game-kit/modules/racing) page has the rest of both starters.

## Show a starter's actors

Some of the platform's starters show actors their server code moves: of the ones Add starter offers, `npcs`, whose mobs, NPCs and pets are its actors, and `ball`, whose ball is one when its Config's `realtime` is on. Such an actor reaches the players near it on the realtime channel, and the SDK draws it like another player's character.

1. In the starter's definition, in the **Game Kit** category, give each kind in **Kit Actors** the actor class it is drawn as, such as `mob` and `BP_Wolf`. `CSO_Kit_Npcs` comes with the kinds `mob`, `npc` and `pet` and no classes. While Kit Actors has a kind, the definition is listed in the **Game Kit Actor Starters** project setting (**Crowdy SDK**, **Server Objects**; `GameKitActorStarters` on `UCrowdySDKDeveloperSettings`), which a packaged game reads, and which cooks the definition and its classes. A kind with no class that loads is not drawn, and the log says so once for that kind.
2. Every actor drawn for a kit actor has a **Crowdy Kit Actor** component, which the SDK adds when the class has none. Its **Kit Actor** is the actor as its hub last showed it: **Kind**, **Id** (its id in its hub, which that hub's calls take), **Instance Id** (the Instance Id of the hub that shows it, such as the area `2,-1` of an `npcs` mob: the Server Object to call for that actor; empty when the hub's actor id does not name one), **Name**, **Hp** and **Max Hp** when **Has Hp** is true, **Mode**, **Owner**, **Location** and **Rotation**. **On Kit Actor Changed** (`OnKitActorChanged`, an `FOnCrowdyKitActorChanged` with the component and the Kit Actor) runs when the actor starts drawing a kit actor and whenever anything but where it is changes, so a health bar or a nameplate listens there; the actor itself moves smoothly to where it is. To bind the event at **Begin Play**, add the component to the class yourself.
3. On a map drawn with Mass, which has no actor per entity, the Crowdy Kit Actor subsystem lists the kit actors this player sees now. **Get Kit Actor Count** (**Kind**) says how many, and **Get Kit Actor** (**Kind**, **Index**, and a **Kit Actor** output) returns true and fills the one at that index, or false when there is none. **Kind** None means every kind. **Get Kit Actors** returns them all as a copy, so a Blueprint that reads every tick, such as a Mass map's, uses the count and **Get Kit Actor**. An actor's index changes as others come and go, so follow an actor by its **Id**, not its index.

A kit actor leaves when its hub says so, or after **Kit Actor Timeout** without an update: 1 second by default, a setting of the map profile's actor management beside the timeout for other actors. A kind that no starter's Kit Actors names reads as None. Positions on the wire are in chunks with z up, which the SDK turns into units by the session's chunk size (`UCrowdyGameSession::SetChunkSize`, 1600 units unless your project changes it). **Chunk Position** (`ChunkPosition`) on a **Kit Actor** holds the raw chunks the hub sent, and **Location** is in world units. In C++ the subsystem's `GetChunkSize()` gives the size it uses, and `PlaceInWorld(KitActor)` turns a Kit Actor's chunks into a place in this world. An update whose **Name** is over 128 bytes or whose **Mode** is over 32 bytes is dropped, and so is one whose **Id**, **Hp**, **Max Hp** or **Owner** is not a whole 64-bit integer.

What the component holds is view state: an actor's health and mode are its hub's to decide, as with any [Server Object](/exec/intro) value. Nothing on the realtime channel proves a hub sent an update, so another player's game can send one under a kit actor's id: show what **Kit Actor** says, and never decide gameplay from it. Call the hub, which checks every call, as **Attack** does.

### The npcs starter's area

A player is in an area while they stand in its 4 by 4 chunks, within one chunk (1600 units) of the area's ground height, so a **Crowdy Server Object** component on the player's pawn holding `CSO_Kit_Npcs`, with its Instance Id set to **This Actor's Area**, follows the area they are in. The ground height is 0 chunks unless the starter's **Config** says otherwise: `{"z": 3}` puts it at 3 chunks, so areas work on a map whose ground is far from Unreal Z 0. `z` is a whole number of chunks within plus or minus 4294967296. The scan counts players within 1 chunk of that height and the area's actors are drawn at it, so give their classes a way to stand on your ground if it is not flat at that height. An area keeps the height it was created with, so changing `z` affects only areas made afterwards.

- Its records are the area's members, one **FCrowdyKitAreaMember** each: its **Type** (**Mob**, **Npc** or **Pet**), **Id**, **X** and **Y** in chunks, and the fields of its type (a mob's **Kind**, **Hp**, **Max Hp** and **Mode**; an NPC's **Name** and **Line**; a pet's **Species**, **Name** and **Mode**). Its State is empty.
- Mobs live 1.5 chunks from the middle of the area (it was 3). So a player standing beside a mob is always in the area, and **Attack** never refuses `not_in_area` there, and one mob is always in view and within day aggro range from anywhere in the area: a player at the centre is always chased. An area saved earlier puts its mobs at the new distance when it loads and keeps its pets.
- **Attack** with a mob's **Id** hits it for 10, once every half second, while the player is within 1.5 chunks of it; its answer is the mob's **Hp**, and at 0 the mob is down (refused with `down`) for 10 seconds. Mobs never hurt players. **Talk** with an NPC's **Id** answers that NPC.
- **Adopt** gives the player a pet where they stand (at most 3 a player and 64 an area), and **RenamePet**, **SummonPet** (it follows them), **DismissPet** and **ReleasePet** take one of their own pets; **GetPets** answers their pets with each one's **Bond**, which only its owner reads.
- A refusal's **Code** is `not_in_area`, `out_of_reach`, `cooldown`, `down`, `limit`, `full` or `forbidden`.

The SDK's `npcs` keeps x and y on the ground and z up, as Unreal does, so its areas and actors are not where the platform's own `npcs` starter, which is y up, puts them; an area the platform's starter saved starts again.

```cpp
#include "Replication/Components/CrowdyKitActorComponent.h"
#include "Replication/Subsystems/CrowdyKitActorSubsystem.h"

// In the wolf's BeginPlay, with the component on the class: its health bar follows its hub.
if (UCrowdyKitActorComponent* Kit = FindComponentByClass<UCrowdyKitActorComponent>())
{
	Kit->OnKitActorChanged.AddDynamic(this, &AWolf::HandleKitActorChanged);
}

// Anywhere in the game world: every mob this player sees now.
const TArray<FCrowdyKitActorState> Mobs = GetWorld()->GetSubsystem<UCrowdyKitActorSubsystem>()->GetKitActors(TEXT("mob"));

// Every tick, without a copy: a reference to the subsystem's own list. A Kind of None is the actors of no named kind.
const TArray<FCrowdyKitActorState>& Wolves = GetWorld()->GetSubsystem<UCrowdyKitActorSubsystem>()->ViewKitActors(TEXT("mob"));
```

### Hit a drawn mob

Two neighbouring areas each number their mobs from 1, so a wolf's **Id** alone does not say which area it belongs to. Its **Instance Id** does: the SDK's `npcs` names each actor `M`, `N` or `P` (mob, NPC or pet), the area's key, `@` and the actor's id, such as `M2,-1@3`, and the SDK reads the area out of it. In Oakford, with `BP_Wolf` given to the `mob` kind and `CSO_Kit_Npcs` added and deployed, a villager hits the wolf they click.

**In Blueprint**, in `BP_Wolf`'s event graph, on a click:

1. Add a **Crowdy Kit Actor** component to `BP_Wolf` in its Components panel, named `CrowdyKitActor`, so the graph can read it. Get its **Kit Actor**, and break it with **Break Crowdy Kit Actor State**. Its **Instance Id** is the wolf's area, such as `2,-1`.
2. Put that **Instance Id** into **Get Server Object** for `CSO_Kit_Npcs`, then **Call Attack (CSO_Kit_Npcs)** on it with **Mob** set to the Kit Actor's **Id**. (Or use a **Crowdy Server Object** component on the wolf holding `CSO_Kit_Npcs`, with its Instance Id set from the same pin.)
3. Under **On Success**, **Hp** is the wolf's health after the hit. At 0 the wolf is down: it stops being drawn and comes back after 10 seconds. Under **On Failed**, **Code** is `out_of_reach` when the villager is too far away, `cooldown` when they hit again within half a second, and `down` when the wolf is already down.

[Blueprint snippet: kit-npc-attack, see /bp/unreal-sdk/kit-npc-attack.txt]

**Talk** works the same with an NPC: its **Instance Id** and **Call Talk (CSO_Kit_Npcs)** with **Npc** set to its **Id**.

**In C++**, with `KitNpcs` a `UPROPERTY` holding `CSO_Kit_Npcs`, in the wolf's click handler:

```cpp
#include "CrowdyGameKitTypes.h"
#include "CrowdyServerObjectSubsystem.h"
#include "Replication/Components/CrowdyKitActorComponent.h"

const UCrowdyKitActorComponent* Kit = FindComponentByClass<UCrowdyKitActorComponent>();
if (!Kit || Kit->KitActor.InstanceId.IsEmpty())
{
	return;
}

FString Error;
UCrowdyServerObject* Area = GetGameInstance()->GetSubsystem<UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem>()->Acquire(KitNpcs, Kit->KitActor.InstanceId, this, Error);
if (!Area)
{
	return;
}
FCrowdyKitAttackInputs Inputs;
Inputs.Mob = Kit->KitActor.Id;
Area->Call(TEXT("Attack"), FInstancedStruct::Make(Inputs), [](const FCrowdyServerCallResult& Result)
{
	if (const FCrowdyKitAttackReply* Reply = Result.Reply.GetPtr<FCrowdyKitAttackReply>())
	{
		UE_LOG(LogTemp, Log, TEXT("The wolf has %d left"), Reply->Hp);
		return;
	}
	UE_CLOG(Result.Code == FName(TEXT("out_of_reach")), LogTemp, Log, TEXT("Too far: %s"), *Result.Reason);
});
```

An empty **Instance Id** means the hub's actor id does not name one, which a kit actor of the SDK's `npcs` never does. An `npcs` added before this keeps the Rust it was added with, and sends the old ids, so **Instance Id** reads empty until you remove it and add it again, or bring the SDK's `Resources/GameKit/npcs/` changes into your `Server/npcs/`, and deploy.

## Change a starter

A starter is a beginning, not a black box, and changing one is changing two things, as for a type of your own:

- **Its Rust.** A starter's server code is `Server/<Type Name>/src/lib.rs`, the starter's own, and the definition asset's [Server Code tab](./write-server-logic.md#the-server-code-tab) shows and edits that file. Its Code Source reads **Game Kit Starter**, which you cannot choose there: only Add starter makes one. **Generate** never writes a starter's code, and refuses with "world is a Game Kit starter: its server code is the starter's own Rust in .../src/lib.rs, which Generate never writes; edit that file". Keep the functions' names and the topics as they are, and the SDK keeps reaching them; the platform's [Your own version of a starter](/game-kit/deploy#your-own-version-of-a-starter) says what to keep.
- **Its definition.** The functions, their inputs and outputs, and the State struct, which you can point at a struct of your own. A **Game Kit** category in Details holds what only starters have: **State Topic**, the topic whose snapshots are the variables (`world`, `queue`, `session`, `board`, `player`, `market`, `duel`, `battle`, `encounter`, `run`, `territory`, `zone`, `table`, `arena`, `race` or `ball`); **Collection Topic** and **Record Struct**, the topic whose members are the records and the struct each one fills (`nodes` and `FCrowdyKitWorldNode` on the world, `area` and `FCrowdyKitAreaMember` on the npcs, empty on the others; a field whose server name is `id` takes the member's id); **Owner Values** and **Keyed By Player**, the [values only the player reads](#values-only-the-player-reads); **Position Function** and **Position Reports Per Second**, the function the component [reports where the player is](#report-where-the-player-is) with, and how often (the arena's are `Move` and 5); **Parent**, the type it runs under (empty makes it the app's root); **Calls**, the types its Rust calls; and **Config**, a JSON object the starter reads when one of its Server Objects first starts, such as `{"day_ms": 600000}` for a ten-minute game day on the world, whose keys are `day_ms`, `nodes`, `node_amount` and `regrow_ms`. Each must be a whole number written without a decimal point or an exponent, from 0, and `nodes` at most 65535. A key in another case, such as `Nodes`, is ignored by the starter and by the check. **Add starter** and **Deploy** refuse a starter Config that holds two ids of one kind (item, currency, faction, counter or pity group) differing only by case, such as `Gold` and `gold`, since the SDK reads them as one. The session's keys are those of `FCrowdyKitSessionConfig` in snake_case (`min_players`, `max_players`, `turn_ms`, `target_score`, `max_rounds`, `lobby_idle_ms`) and `board`. The player's Config, one for every player, sets the inventory's `slots`, the `items` catalog, the soft `currencies`, the `start` bundle a new player gets, `levels`, `achievements`, `quests`, the `client_counters` a client may report with **Step**, `recipes`, `loot` tables, idle `production` and a `secret` mixed into loot rolls; the market's sets its `vendors`, the `book_items` with an order book, the `book_currency` they trade in, `orders_max` per player, `trade_ms` before an open trade is called off, and `player_type`, the Type Name of the players' hubs. The platform's [Player](/game-kit/modules/player) and [Market](/game-kit/modules/market) pages give each key's shape. The deck's Config holds one key, the `secret` mixed into every shuffle, which **Add starter** writes for you, as it writes the player's. The matchmaker and leaderboard read no Config. The other game-mode starters do: the minigame's `best_of` and `round_ms`; the tactics battle's board size, obstacles, phase time and units; the director's field, lane, towers, `kinds`, `waves` and `boss`; the instance's `floors`, `rooms_per_floor`, `party_max`, `idle_ms` and `board`; the territory's `points`, `factions`, `win_score` and income; the zone's `center`, `radius`, `phases` and damage; and the arena's `origin`, `width`, `depth`, `spawns`, `classes`, `players_max`, `kill_target`, `duration_ms`, `respawn_ms`, `teleport`, `remove_after_strikes`, `idle_ms`, `realtime` and `height`, as [Fight in an arena](#fight-in-an-arena) lists them; the race's and the ball's are in [Run a race](#run-a-race) and [Play a ball game](#play-a-ball-game). The platform's [Duels](/game-kit/modules/minigames), [Grid battles](/game-kit/modules/tactics), [Waves and bosses](/game-kit/modules/encounters), [Private runs](/game-kit/modules/instances) and [Control points and circles](/game-kit/modules/territory) and [Arenas](/game-kit/modules/arenas) pages give each one's shape. The settings a starter's Rust decides for itself are hidden: Readable By, the visibility of each variable, Members, Records, Timers and Events, Callable By, Required Feature and Cooldown.

![The Game Kit category of CSO_Kit_Npcs: its Collection Topic area and Record Struct, Kit Actors with the mob, npc and pet kinds, Parent world, Calls world and an empty Config](/img/unreal-sdk/kit-starter-definition.png)

On the Overview tab a changed starter reads **Changed** like any type, and lists `config` or `parent` when those changed. A starter is marked **Problem** when its Config is not a JSON object ("its Game Kit Config is not a JSON object; correct it, or empty it to keep the starter's defaults"), when two starters have no Parent ("... an app has one root; give one of them a Parent"), when Parents run in a circle, when a Parent is a Server Function Library, when the `world` starter's Config has a `day_ms`, `nodes`, `node_amount` or `regrow_ms` that is not a whole number as described above, or when a `player` or `deck` starter's `secret` is not a string, since the starter would refuse its Config. A problem blocks Deploy. Deploy also warns, without stopping, when a `player` or `deck` starter's Config has no `secret` (exactly that key, a non-empty string), since players could then work out loot rolls and deals; see [Play a card game with a hidden hand](#play-a-card-game-with-a-hidden-hand). It is marked **Needs generating** when its crate lost a file: "its server code is missing ...; restore it, or remove the type in Server Compute and add the starter again".

## Remove starters

From the command line, `-op=removestarter` takes a starter out of the project together with every starter that runs under it or calls it:

```text
UnrealEditor-Cmd.exe Oakford.uproject -run=CrowdyServerCompute -op=removestarter -starter=world -yes
```

Removing `world` removes every starter, since they all run under it; removing `matchmaker` removes the matchmaker and the session; removing `session` removes the session and the matchmaker, which calls it, and leaves the leaderboard; removing `leaderboard` removes only the leaderboard, since a session or an instance calls it only when its Config names a board; removing `player` removes the player and the market, which calls it, and removing `market` removes only the market. Removing `npcs`, `minigame`, `tactics`, `director`, `instance`, `territory`, `zone`, `deck` or `arena` removes only that starter, since nothing else calls it. For each it deletes the definition and the whole `Server/<Type Name>/` folder, your edits to its Rust included, and it clears **Game Kit World** when that named one of them. Without `-yes` it names what it would remove and changes nothing: "This deletes session, matchmaker, world: their definitions and server code, edits included; pass -yes to confirm." It logs "removing session, matchmaker, world", then "removed session, matchmaker, world", and its result has `starter` and `removed`.

Before it changes anything it checks, and removes nothing when:

- a starter's folder is, or holds, a link to somewhere else, since deleting it could delete what the link points to;
- a definition's file is read-only, for example checked in to source control without a checkout: make it writable and run it again;
- something else uses a definition, such as a Blueprint whose Server Object component holds `CSO_Kit_World`: the message names each one, so remove those uses first.

If a file is held open while it deletes, for example by an IDE or `cargo`, the message ends "run -op=removestarter again to finish", which picks up where it stopped.

On the Server Compute page, **Remove from project...** in a starter's **...** menu does the same, after a dialog naming every starter it removes and what the next deploy takes offline.

Either way the server is not touched until your next deploy, which no longer sends the starters and removes them. Without `world`, that deploy sends the Crowdy SDK's root again, and **Get Players Online** reads it.

## In the example world

Oakford's lantern posts light up at night, and villagers gather oil from the world's resource nodes. Add the `world` starter on Server Compute and deploy.

**In Blueprint**, in the lantern post's event graph:

1. Add **On bNight Changed (CSO_Kit_World)** and wire **Event BeginPlay** into its **Bind**. It needs no Target, since the world has Only One Instance. From **Changed**, wire its **bNight** to **Set Visibility** on the lantern's Point Light component, named `Light`.
2. To gather oil, add **Call Harvest (CSO_Kit_World)** and set its **Node** input to a node's id. The world's resource nodes are its records: from the world's **Get Server Object**, **Get Record Ids** lists them, and **Watch Record** on the oil node's id runs with what it holds now and after each harvest, read with **Find Record** and **Get Server Value** `Amount`.
3. Under **On Success**, its **Left** output is what the node holds now. Under **On Failed**, show the arrow at the bottom of the node: **Outcome** is **Denied** with **Retryable** true and **Code** `cooldown` when the villager harvests too soon, and **Refused** with **Code** `empty` when the node is bare until it grows back.

[Blueprint snippet: kit-world-night, see /bp/unreal-sdk/kit-world-night.txt]

[Blueprint snippet: kit-world-harvest, see /bp/unreal-sdk/kit-world-harvest.txt]

**In C++**, with `KitWorld` a `UPROPERTY` holding `CSO_Kit_World`, the lantern post follows the night and a villager harvests:

```cpp
#include "CrowdyGameKitTypes.h"
#include "CrowdyServerObjectSubsystem.h"

FString Error;
UCrowdyServerObject* World = GetGameInstance()->GetSubsystem<UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem>()->Acquire(KitWorld, FString(), this, Error);
World->WatchValues(FOnCrowdyServerValuesChanged::FDelegate::CreateWeakLambda(this, [this](UCrowdyServerObject* Object, TConstArrayView<FString> Fields)
{
	const FCrowdyKitWorldConditions* Conditions = Object->GetState().GetPtr<FCrowdyKitWorldConditions>();
	if (Conditions && Fields.Contains(TEXT("night")))
	{
		Light->SetVisibility(Conditions->bNight);
	}
}));

FCrowdyKitHarvestInputs Inputs;
Inputs.Node = NodeId;
World->Call(TEXT("Harvest"), FInstancedStruct::Make(Inputs), [](const FCrowdyServerCallResult& Result)
{
	if (const FCrowdyKitHarvestReply* Reply = Result.Reply.GetPtr<FCrowdyKitHarvestReply>())
	{
		UE_LOG(LogTemp, Log, TEXT("The node holds %d"), Reply->Left);
		return;
	}
	UE_CLOG(Result.Code == FName(TEXT("empty")), LogTemp, Log, TEXT("Bare until it grows back: %s"), *Result.Reason);
});

const FString OilNode = LexToString(NodeId);
World->WatchRecord(OilNode, FOnCrowdyServerValuesChanged::FDelegate::CreateWeakLambda(this, [OilNode](UCrowdyServerObject* Object, TConstArrayView<FString> Fields)
{
	const FCrowdyServerRecord* Record = Object->GetRecord(OilNode);
	const FCrowdyKitWorldNode* Node = Record ? Record->Values.GetPtr<FCrowdyKitWorldNode>() : nullptr;
	UE_CLOG(Node != nullptr, LogTemp, Log, TEXT("Oil left: %d"), Node ? Node->Amount : 0);
}));
```

The world's Instance Id is ignored, since it has Only One Instance. For a match, [Find Match](#find-a-match-and-take-turns) on `CSO_Kit_Matchmaker` with the queue's name, then acquire `CSO_Kit_Session` with its **Session Id** and call **Join**, **Ready** and, as its host, **Start**; **Is My Turn** says when to let the player move.

Oakford also keeps a weekly board of its lantern-lighting matches. Add the `leaderboard` starter, set `CSO_Kit_Session`'s **Config** to `{"board": "oakford-weekly"}` and deploy. Each finished match then posts its players' scores, and the village notice board, with `KitLeaderboard` a `UPROPERTY` holding `CSO_Kit_Leaderboard`, reads them:

```cpp
UCrowdyServerObject* Board = GetGameInstance()->GetSubsystem<UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem>()->Acquire(KitLeaderboard, TEXT("oakford-weekly"), this, Error);
Board->Call(TEXT("GetMyPlace"), FInstancedStruct(), [](const FCrowdyServerCallResult& Result)
{
	const FCrowdyKitBoardPlace* Place = Result.Reply.GetPtr<FCrowdyKitBoardPlace>();
	UE_CLOG(Place && Place->Rank > 0, LogTemp, Log, TEXT("This week you are number %d with %lld"), Place ? Place->Rank : 0, Place ? Place->Score : 0LL);
});
```

In Blueprint the same is **Call GetMyPlace (CSO_Kit_Leaderboard)** on the object **Get Server Object** gives for `CSO_Kit_Leaderboard` and `oakford-weekly`, with its **Rank** and **Score** outputs; **On Top Changed (CSO_Kit_Leaderboard)** keeps the notice board's top ten current.

At Oakford's market, villagers swap the planks they craft for gold. Add the `market` starter, which brings `player`, and deploy. [Trade between two players](#trade-between-two-players) is the whole exchange; in C++, with `KitMarket` and `KitPlayer` `UPROPERTY`s holding `CSO_Kit_Market` and `CSO_Kit_Player`, the villager selling planks offers them on the trade the buyer opened, and the purse follows the wallet:

```cpp
UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem* Objects = GetGameInstance()->GetSubsystem<UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem>();
UCrowdyServerObject* Market = Objects->Acquire(KitMarket, TEXT("oakford"), this, Error);
UCrowdyServerObject* Me = Objects->Acquire(KitPlayer, FString(), this, Error);

FCrowdyKitTradeOfferInputs Offer;
Offer.Trade = TradeId;
Offer.Bundle.Items.Add(TEXT("plank"), 3);
Market->Call(TEXT("Offer"), FInstancedStruct::Make(Offer), [](const FCrowdyServerCallResult& Result)
{
	UE_CLOG(!Result.IsSuccess(), LogTemp, Log, TEXT("Not offered: %s (%s)"), *Result.Code.ToString(), *Result.Reason);
});

Me->WatchValues(FOnCrowdyServerValuesChanged::FDelegate::CreateWeakLambda(this, [this](UCrowdyServerObject* Object, TConstArrayView<FString> Fields)
{
	const FCrowdyKitPlayerState* State = Object->GetState().GetPtr<FCrowdyKitPlayerState>();
	if (State && Fields.Contains(TEXT("wallet")))
	{
		Purse->SetText(FText::AsNumber(State->Wallet.Balances.FindRef(TEXT("gold"))));
	}
}));
```

In Blueprint the offer is **Call Offer (CSO_Kit_Market)** with the trade's id and a **Bundle** made with **Make Crowdy Kit Bundle**, and the purse is **On Wallet Changed (CSO_Kit_Player)** on the player's own `CSO_Kit_Player`. Gold here is in-game soft currency, never real money.

# Keep a record per entity

:::note
Server Objects run on [ck-exec](/exec/intro). Records per entity are new in v2.19.0; see [What's Changed](../guides/whats-changed.md#2026-10-12-sdk-v2190).
:::

A crowd of raiders on Oakford's north road is hundreds of entities, and each one has values the server must own: how hurt it is, what it carries, who hired it. A Server Object with **Holds** set to **A Record per Entity** keeps those values once for each entity, in one object, and tells each player only what that player may see.

## When to use it

Use a record per entity when:

- you have many entities, such as a crowd, a warband or a field of crops, each with the same few values;
- the values are the server's to decide, so they belong in a [Server Object](./overview.md) and not in [Crowdy State](../runtime/crowdy-state.md);
- players should see some values of an entity and others only for their own entities or their team's.

The object that holds the records is a shared one: one for an area of the world, for a match or for a team. It is not one object per entity. An app can run at most 1,024 instances at once (see [Operations](/exec/operations#instances-and-versions)), so a Server Object for each of two thousand raiders cannot work, while one object per area holding their records can.

A thing that has one set of values, such as a boss, a beacon or a player's stash, stays a normal Server Object. See [Relate Server Objects to each other](./relate-server-objects.md).

The worked version of this page is [Raiders on the north road](./examples/raiders-on-the-north-road.md).

## Make the type

Make the asset as on [Create a Server Object type](./create-a-type.md), then select **Type Settings** and set:

| Setting | Where | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Holds** | Server Object | **One Set of Variables** | Choose **A Record per Entity**. The variables become the fields of each record. |
| **Visible to Players** | Each variable's Details | | **Server Only**, **Everyone**, **Owner Only** or **Team Only**. See [Who receives a value](#who-receives-a-value). |
| **Max Records** | Records | 2048 | The most records one object keeps, 1 to 4096. |
| **Send Changes Every** | Records | 250 ms | How often the server sends what changed, 50 to 5000 ms. |
| **Team From** | Records | **This Object's Records** | Where a team comes from: **This Object's Records** or **Crowdy Team**. See [Who receives a value](#who-receives-a-value). |
| **Area Size** | Areas | 4 | The side of one area, in chunks, 1 to 64. See [Pick the object](#pick-the-object). |
| **Areas Followed Around a Player** | Areas | 1 | How many areas out from a player's own are followed, 0 to 3. 1 follows the 3 by 3 around it. |
| **Reads World Data** | Platform Data | Off | Lets the server code ask which actors are near a chunk. Deploying asks for the `world.read` permission. |

![The raider type with Type Settings selected: Holds set to A Record per Entity, then the Records category (Max Records, Send Changes Every, Team From) and the Areas category (Area Size, Areas Followed Around a Player)](/img/unreal-sdk/server-object-records.png)

**Readable By** must be **Every Player**: who sees which variable is decided per variable, not for the whole object. **Members From** must be **None**, since each record carries its own owner and team. Saving the asset says so when either is wrong.

**Area Size** and **Areas Followed Around a Player** work for every type, whatever it holds. **Reads World Data** does too.

A type of this kind starts with no records. The server code adds them.

## Who receives a value

Every variable of a record has one audience:

| Audience | Who receives it |
|---|---|
| **Server Only** | Nobody. It stays on the server. |
| **Everyone** | Every player who watches the object. |
| **Owner Only** | The player who owns the record's entity. |
| **Team Only** | A player whose own record in this object has the same team as this record. |

![Loot selected on the raider type: Variable Name, Variable Type, Visible to Players set to Owner Only and the Default Value](/img/unreal-sdk/server-object-variable-audience.png)

Each record has an **owner**, a player's user id or 0 for none, and a **team**, a string. Your server code sets both. They are not variables of your struct, and no player can set them.

Which players count as the record's team depends on **Team From**:

- **This Object's Records**, the default. A player's team in an object is the team of a record that player owns in that same object. A player who owns several records in one object uses the first by id. An empty team matches nothing. The team is a string you choose, such as `ravens`.
- **Crowdy Team**. A record's team is the id of a [Crowdy Team](../services/teams.md), written in digits, and a player is on it when the platform says they are a member of that team. `insert` and `set_team` refuse a team that is not digits; an empty team means no team. Deploying asks for the `players.read` permission, since the server checks membership through the platform.

Choose by how far a team reaches. With **This Object's Records** a player's team is known only inside one object. When records are kept per area, that means Team Only values reach a teammate only while both players are in the same area. If teams must see each other's values across areas, use **Crowdy Team**.

A player who owns no record in the object and is on no matching team sees the Everyone values and nothing else.

How the values travel depends on the audience:

- **Everyone values** come in batches. The server collects the changes and, every **Send Changes Every**, sends the records that changed, each with only the Everyone variables that changed. A record that changes twice in between is sent once with its last value. A record you remove is removed on every player's side with the same batch.
- **Owner Only and Team Only values** are fetched. The batch carries a notice that some changed, and the owner's or team's game asks the server for them, at most once a second for each object. They arrive a moment after the Everyone values.
- **A player who joins, or whose game missed a batch,** reads every record again, a page at a time.

A value a player does not receive reads as the variable's default, and raises no change. When a record's owner or team changes, the Owner Only and Team Only values of that record go back to their defaults for everyone who just lost access. When a player's own team changes, their game reads the object again.

## Pick the object

The component on an actor, and a crowd row, choose which object holds the records. For a crowd the usual choice is the area the entity stands in.

- **Only One Instance** (a setting of the type): one object for the whole world. Only for a small number of entities.
- **Instance Id**: a name you choose, such as `match-7`, for a match or a team.
- **This Actor's Area**: the object for the area the actor stands in, which changes as the actor moves.

**This Actor's Area** cuts the ground into squares of **Area Size** by **Area Size** chunks. The object's Instance Id is the square's `x,y`: the chunk's x and y, which are Unreal's X and Y over the session's chunk size, each divided by Area Size and rounded down, so the area at chunk (-1, 5) with an Area Size of 4 is `-1,1`. The height is not part of the key. The component checks every half second and, when the actor crosses into another area, joins that area's object and lets go of the old one after a short grace period, so an actor on a border does not flap.

Moving a record from one area's object to the next is your server code's job. One approach: the census in each area adds the entities it finds standing there and removes the ones that left, as the code below does.

## Write the server code

Generate the type as on [Write its server logic](./write-server-logic.md). In `logic.rs` the functions are implemented on `Records`, the object's records, and not on the state struct:

```rust
impl Functions for Records {
    fn skirmish(&mut self, _ctx: &Ctx) -> Result<()> {
        let ids: Vec<String> = self.ids().map(str::to_string).collect();
        for id in ids {
            let Some(raider) = self.get_mut(&id) else { continue };
            raider.Health -= 5;
            if raider.Health <= 0 {
                self.remove(&id);
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}
```

`Records` gives your code:

| Name | What it does |
|---|---|
| `get(id)` | The record's values, or `None`. |
| `get_mut(id)` | The record's values to change, or `None`. |
| `insert(id, owner, team, state)` | Adds the record, or replaces the one with that id. Returns an error past **Max Records** (`records_full`), for an id that is empty or over 64 bytes, for a team over 64 bytes, and for either holding a control character (`bad_record`). |
| `remove(id)` | Removes the record and returns its values. |
| `set_owner(id, owner)`, `set_team(id, team)` | Change who owns the record and which team it is on. An error (`bad_record`) when there is no such record. |
| `owner(id)`, `team(id)` | The record's owner (0 for none) and team. |
| `ids()`, `len()` | Every id in order, and how many records there are. |
| `owned_by(player)` | The ids of the records a player owns. |
| `Records::area_of(chunk_x, chunk_y)` | The Instance Id of the area holding a chunk, worked out with this type's **Area Size**: the same key the component uses. |

Changes made in one call are kept or put back together, like the variables of any Server Object: a function that returns an error leaves the records as they were. Ids are text, 1 to 64 bytes, and compared exactly, capitals included.

### Count who stands in the area

To add the entities standing in an area, turn on **Reads World Data** and ask the world from a timer. `actors(at)` returns the actors in one chunk, and each one's `uuid` is the id its record uses. Ask for each chunk of the area in turn:

```rust
fn census(&mut self, ctx: &Ctx) -> Result<()> {
    let Some((x, y)) = area_origin(&ctx.key) else { return Ok(()) };
    for cx in x..x + AREA_SIZE {
        for cy in y..y + AREA_SIZE {
            let Ok(actors) = ctx.world().actors(ChunkPos::new(cx, cy, 0)) else { return Ok(()) };
            for actor in actors {
                if self.get(&actor.uuid).is_none() {
                    let _ = self.insert(&actor.uuid, 0, "", RaiderState::default());
                }
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

Three things to know about the census:

- **It reads the heights it asks for.** The call above reads ground level, height 0, and sees nothing standing higher. Ask for more heights if your world has them.
- **One call answers up to 200 actors.** A chunk with 200 in the answer may hold more. Do not remove a record because its actor is missing from an answer that is full: skip the removals for that tick.
- **Do not use `actors_near` for a dense crowd.** It answers at most 500 actors across several chunks, so a crowded area is cut short, and records of live entities would look as if they had left.

`area_origin` turns the `x,y` in `ctx.key` back into the area's first chunk. [Raiders on the north road](./examples/raiders-on-the-north-road.md) shows the whole census, with the removal of the entities that left. The platform's pages on these calls are [World and platform data](/exec/world-and-platform-data).

## From Blueprint

Put a **Crowdy Server Object** component on the actor, with **Definition** set to the type and **Instance Mode** set to **This Actor's Area**, **Instance Id** or the type's single instance. The actor needs a **Crowdy Entity** component as well: its id names the actor's record. Without one the component fails with "needs a Crowdy Entity component on this actor".

![The Crowdy Server Object component: Definition CSO_Raider and Instance Mode This Actor's Area](/img/unreal-sdk/server-object-component-area.png)

The component reads **its actor's own record**, so the nodes work as they do for any Server Object. The typed **Get** and **On Changed** nodes, **On Variables Changed**, **Get Variables** and **Get Server Value** all give that record's values, and **Changed** runs when one of them changes. See [Read and follow its variables](./from-blueprint/read-and-follow-variables.md).

To reach other entities' records, take the Server Object from the component with **Get Server Object**, and use these nodes on it:

| Node | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Find Record** | Takes an **Id** and gives that record's **Values**, its **Owner** and its **Team**. Returns false when there is no such record. Read a variable out of **Values** with **Get Server Value**. |
| **Get Record Ids** | The id of every record the player can see, in no particular order. |
| **Holds Records** | True when the type keeps a record per entity rather than one set of variables. |
| **Num Records** | How many records the object holds now. |
| **Watch Record** | Takes a **Record Id** and an event (the delegate `FCrowdyServerRecordEvent`). The event runs with the **Object**, the **Record Id** and the **Changed** variables after each change of that record, and when it is removed (**Find Record** is then false). When the record is already current it runs once at once, naming all its variables. A watch added from an **On Records Changed** or **On Records Updated** handler gets that catch-up and does not run a second time for the same change. Records dropped because the object **Failed** are not reported; **On Status Changed** is. |
| **Stop Watching Record** | Takes the same **Record Id** and event, and stops that watch. |
| **On Records Changed** | An event that runs after every read, batch or fetch that changed records, with the **Object**, the ids that are new or changed, and the ids that were removed. |
| **On Records Updated** | Like **On Records Changed**, but each changed record comes with its changed variables: a list of **Record Id** and **Changed Variables**, then the removed ids. **Changed Variables** is empty when only the record's owner or team changed. |

**Watch Record** works like **Watch Message**: get the Server Object from the component, call **Watch Record** with a **Record Id**, and wire a custom event with the delegate's pins (the **Object**, the **Record Id** and the **Changed** variables) to its **Event**. The figure follows one record, here an id `raider-1` that your server code inserted, and prints its id each time it changes.

[Blueprint snippet: so-record-watch, see /bp/unreal-sdk/so-record-watch.txt]

Pan a Blueprint figure with the right mouse button. Press **Copy nodes**, then Ctrl+V in your own event graph.

**Stop Watching Record** takes the same **Record Id** and the same event:

[Blueprint snippet: so-record-unwatch, see /bp/unreal-sdk/so-record-unwatch.txt]

**On Records Updated** is bound from **Begin Play** like any event of the object, with **Bind Event to On Records Updated** on the Server Object. Its event gets the changed records, each with its **Record Id** and **Changed Variables**, and the removed ids. The figure prints how many records changed:

[Blueprint snippet: so-records-updated, see /bp/unreal-sdk/so-records-updated.txt]

**Find By Asset** and the by-name **Watch Variables** read a type of this kind as if it had no records, so they give its defaults. Use the component, or **Find Record**.

## From C++

The component works as in Blueprint, and its `GetVariables()` and `OnVariablesChanged` give the actor's own record. To read others, acquire the area's object yourself. `FindGroundChunk` gives the chunk under a location as actor updates place it, and `GetAreaKey` on the definition turns it into the Instance Id:

```cpp
UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem* Objects = GetGameInstance()->GetSubsystem<UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem>();
int64 ChunkX = 0;
int64 ChunkY = 0;
if (!Objects || !Objects->FindGroundChunk(GetActorLocation(), ChunkX, ChunkY))
{
	return;
}
FString Error;
UCrowdyServerObject* Area = Objects->Acquire(RaiderDefinition, RaiderDefinition->GetAreaKey(ChunkX, ChunkY), this, Error);
if (Area)
{
	Area->OnRecordsChanged.AddUObject(this, &ARoadWatch::HandleRecords);
}
```

`OnRecordsChanged`, an `FOnCrowdyServerRecordsChanged`, runs after every read, batch or fetch that changed records. It gives the object, the ids that are new or changed, the ids removed, and for each changed id the State fields that changed, one bit per field. Read a record with `GetRecord`, which returns an `FCrowdyServerRecord` holding the `Values`, the `Owner` and the `Team`:

```cpp
void ARoadWatch::HandleRecords(UCrowdyServerObject* Object, TConstArrayView<FString> Changed, TConstArrayView<FString> Removed, TConstArrayView<TBitArray<>> ChangedFields)
{
	for (const FString& Id : Changed)
	{
		const FCrowdyServerRecord* Record = Object->GetRecord(Id);
		const FRaiderState* Raider = Record ? Record->Values.GetPtr<FRaiderState>() : nullptr;
		if (Raider)
		{
			UpdateBar(Id, Raider->Health);
		}
	}
}
```

`HoldsRecords()` says whether the definition keeps records, `NumRecords()` counts them, `GetRecordIds()` lists them, and `WatchRecord(Id, Listener)` follows one record, naming its variables on each change. Ids are compared with capitals significant. `WatchValues` and `GetState()` on a records object give the type's defaults, not a record.

The rest of the C++ surface for records:

- `UCrowdyServerObject::FindValues` gives a record's values by id, or the object's own when the id is null. The Blueprint **On Records Changed** is the `RecordsChanged` property, an `FCrowdyServerRecordsChanged`, and **On Records Updated** is the `RecordsUpdated` property, an `FCrowdyServerRecordsUpdated` whose `FCrowdyServerRecordChange` entries hold a `RecordId` and its `ChangedVariables`.
- `UCrowdyServerObjectComponent::ReadsRecord` says whether the component reads its actor's record, and `UCrowdyServerObjectComponent::GetRecordId` gives that record's id.
- `UCrowdyServerObjectDefinition::GetAreaKey` and the static `UCrowdyServerObjectDefinition::MakeAreaKey` turn a chunk into an area's Instance Id, and `UCrowdyServerObjectDefinition::HasCrowdyTeamRecords` says whether the type keeps records on Crowdy Teams. `UCrowdyServerObjectSubsystem::GetChunkSize` gives the chunk size the game session uses.
- The settings are typed by `ECrowdyServerObjectHolds`, `ECrowdyServerFieldAudience` (**Everyone**, **Owner Only**, **Team Only**) and `ECrowdyServerTeamSource` (**This Object's Records**, **Crowdy Team**).

## Crowd rows

A crowd drawn with the Crowdy Mass Rendering Backend has no actor for most entities, only rows, but a row reads the same records. See [Draw a crowd with Mass](../mass/draw-a-crowd-with-mass.md). The record's id is the entity's id, the 32 characters the census calls report as `uuid`, so a census that keys records by the actor's `uuid` lands each record on its row.

- Set the **Crowdy Server Object** component's **Instance Mode** on the entity's actor class to **Only One Instance**, **Instance Id** with an id typed, or **This Actor's Area**. With any other mode the rows get no server values, and the log says why once for the class.
- Read the values with [Read its server values, from Blueprint](../mass/from-blueprint/read-its-server-values.md) or [Read its server values, from C++](../mass/from-cpp/read-its-server-values.md).

## Size it

What a player receives is the changed records, times the bytes of each record, times the sends a second:

> 500 records changed in a send, 20 bytes each, 4 sends a second: about 40 KB a second to each player.

- **Raise Send Changes Every** when records change more than once in between: the last value is the only one sent. Lower it for faster feel, at the cost of more sends.
- **Keep records small.** Put a value in a record only if a player or the server logic needs it per entity.
- **Max Records** is 2048 unless you change it, and 4096 at most. A call that inserts past it is refused with `records_full`.
- A client refuses a message over 1 MiB. The server splits a batch at 4,096 records or 512 KiB into several sends, in order, and a player's first read comes in pages of up to 512 KiB.
- A player on a slow link falls behind and catches up. Batches queue instead of dropping.
- Size the area so one object holds the records you want its watchers to receive. A player follows `(2r+1)` squared objects, with `r` the **Areas Followed Around a Player**: 9 by default.

## Limits and rules

- **Readable By** must be **Every Player**, and **Members From** must be **None**.
- A variable's audience is set per variable, and only a variable that is **Visible to Players** can be Owner Only or Team Only. A variable cannot be both. Saving checks both.
- The function name `changes` and a timer whose name in snake case is `crowdy_flush` or `crowdy_sessions` are reserved.
- A record's id is 1 to 64 bytes of text with no control characters. Ids that differ only in capitals are different records.
- A team is at most 64 bytes. With **Team From** on **Crowdy Team** it is a team id in digits or empty, and anything else is refused.
- A type starts with no records, so a seed must be empty.
- The owner and team are set by server code only.

## If a value is not what you expect

- **Always the default.** A player gets Owner Only values only for records they own, and Team Only values only when their own record in the object has the same team. A player who owns no record sees neither.
- **A Team Only value arrives late.** Owner Only and Team Only values are fetched, at most once a second for each object.
- **The component stays Connecting or fails.** See [Troubleshooting](./troubleshooting.md#a-server-object-does-not-become-ready). A record type needs its census or other server code to add records first.
- **To see the pushes and fetches,** turn on [`crowdy.exec.trace`](./troubleshooting.md#seeing-what-a-server-object-is-doing).

# Make a Server Function Library
Source: https://docs.crowdedkingdoms.com/unreal-sdk/exec/server-function-libraries

# Make a Server Function Library

:::note
Server Objects run on [ck-exec](/exec/intro).
:::

A **Server Function Library** is a Server Object type that has functions and nothing else. It is the server's counterpart of a Blueprint Function Library: a place for logic that many callers share, with no instance of its own to hold. You describe it in the same [definition asset](./create-a-type.md) as any Server Object type, choose **Kind: Server Function Library**, and the editor hides what it cannot have.

|  | Server Object | Server Function Library |
|---|---|---|
| Keeps variables, saved between calls | Yes | No |
| Instances | One for each Instance Id | One name for everyone; the server runs several instances of it and picks one for each call |
| Calls answered at the same time | One for each Server Object | Several for each instance, on several instances |
| Players watch its values, hear its messages | Yes | No: there is nothing to watch |
| Members, timers, records, messages, cooldowns | Yes | No |
| Players call its functions | Yes | Yes |
| Other server code calls its functions | Yes | Yes |

## When to use one

Use a library for a rule or a calculation that has no memory of its own and that several callers need: turning a seed into a loot drop, pricing an item, checking whether a move is legal, scoring a result. Because several instances answer at once, a busy game keeps getting answers where one Server Object would make its callers queue. A library can also change game truth, but only by calling a Server Object: a library function that rewards a player calls the player's own Server Object and that object changes its values.

Do not use one for anything that has to remember. A counter, a stockpile, a match or a record per player belongs in a [Server Object](./create-a-type.md), because a library saves nothing: each instance runs your code in several copies, each with its own memory, so a value a function leaves in a variable can be there for one call and gone for the next. A library has no [timers](./access-members-and-timers.md#timers-and-events) and cannot [listen to messages](./access-members-and-timers.md#listen-to-other-server-objects), and a player cannot watch it; whatever players should see goes in a Server Object that the library's caller changes.

Two things to plan for:

- **The first call after the library has been idle can take over a second.** Players wait a little; server code calling it may be told the call timed out. See [A call to a library from server code fails](./troubleshooting.md#a-call-to-a-library-from-server-code-fails).
- **Each instance counts toward the app's limit on running Server Objects**, 16 for an app with no funds and 16,384 for a funded one. A library that keeps 4 instances running leaves an unfunded app room for 12 other Server Objects. See [Scaling](#scaling).

## In the example world

The Hollow Giant should drop something when it falls, and a chest on the north road should hand out a trinket. Both want the same rule: turn a seed and a tier into an item name. That rule keeps nothing, so it is a library, `CSO_LootRoller`, with one function, `RollLoot`. The chest calls it from a Blueprint or from C++, and the boss's server code calls it too. [Loot for the Hollow Giant](./examples/hollow-giant-loot.md) builds all of it. This page makes the asset.

## Make the asset

Make a **Crowdy Server Object Definition** as on [Create a Server Object type](./create-a-type.md#make-the-asset) and name it `CSO_LootRoller`. Select **Type Settings**, the first row of the Server Object panel. In Details, under **Server Object**:

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Type Name | `loot_roller` |
| Kind | **Server Function Library** |
| Description | Turns a seed and a tier into an item name. |

**Kind** is the second row, after **Type Name**. It is **Server Object** until you change it, and changing it rebuilds Details at once. The Server Object panel then shows **Type Settings** and **Functions**, and no Variables or Server Messages. Add the function `RollLoot` with **+** in **Functions**, as on [Create a Server Object type](./create-a-type.md#make-the-asset): **Callable By** on **Players**, and two inputs, `Seed` (Integer64) and `Tier` (Integer, starting at 1, with a **Value Range** of 0 to 4), and one output, `Item` (String). Or use structs, as the [boss](./examples/shared-boss.md#what-the-server-keeps) does.

![The CSO_LootRoller editor: the Server Object panel lists only Type Settings and Functions, and RollLoot reads (Seed, Tier) returns Item](/img/unreal-sdk/server-function-library-editor.png)

A library has these settings, and no others:

- **Type Name**, **Kind** and **Description**.
- **Functions**, with their **Name**, **Callable By**, **Required Feature**, inputs, outputs, **Value Range** and **Server Name**. **Callable By** can be **Players**, **Server Only** or **Developers Only**; see [Callable By](./create-a-type.md#callable-by).
- **Platform Data**, as on [Access, members and timers](./access-members-and-timers.md#platform-data), for what the library's server code reads and changes of players, the world and grids.
- **Can Call** and **Can Call Any Type**, the types its server code may call; see [Call other Server Objects](./access-members-and-timers.md#call-other-server-objects).
- **Scaling**, below.
- **Server Limits**, collapsed, and **Server Names**, in the Advanced group, as on [Every setting](./create-a-type.md#every-setting).

Everything else is hidden in Details and ignored everywhere, even if the asset still holds values from when it was a Server Object: it is not checked when you save, not in the generated server code, not deployed and not used by the game. That is the variables or State struct, **Visible to Players**, **Readable By**, **Only One Instance**, **Holds** and its records, **Members From** and the other member settings, **Areas**, **Timers**, **On Player Joined** and **On Player Left**, **Server Messages**, **Listen To**, **Save Every** and **Stop When Idle For**.

Nothing picks an instance of a library, so a caller gives no Instance Id. A library whose functions are all **Server Only** is [closed to players](./access-members-and-timers.md#a-type-players-cannot-reach): only other server code and developer tools reach it. That is what a library that only your Server Objects use should be.

## Scaling

The **Scaling** category, shown only for a library, says how many instances the server runs and how many calls each answers at once. The platform term is in brackets.

![With Type Settings selected, Details of CSO_LootRoller shows Type Name, Kind set to Server Function Library and Description, then Platform Data, Can Call and the Scaling category: Min Instances 1, Max Instances 1 and Parallel Calls per Instance 16](/img/unreal-sdk/server-function-library-type.png)

| Setting | C++ | Range | Default | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Min Instances** [`replicas`] | `MinInstances` | 1 to 64 | 1 | How many instances run all the time. |
| **Max Instances** [`max_replicas`] | `MaxInstances` | 1 to 64 | 1 | The most the server adds when calls wait. Equal to Min Instances, it never adds any. |
| **Parallel Calls per Instance** [`concurrency`] | `ParallelCallsPerInstance` | 1 to 256 | 16 | How many calls one instance answers at the same time. |

The server adds an instance, up to **Max Instances**, once calls have waited 2 seconds for an answer, and removes an added instance after about 30 seconds in which it is nearly idle. It never goes below **Min Instances**. So the library answers at least Min Instances times Parallel Calls per Instance calls at once, and more when it has grown.

Start with the defaults. Raise **Max Instances** when bursts of callers wait, **Min Instances** to have those instances ready before the burst, and **Parallel Calls per Instance** to let each instance answer more at once. The longest one call may run is still the type's **Time per Call**, 2 seconds unless you change it, and a call that runs past it is answered **Timeout** like any other; see [Server Limits](./create-a-type.md#every-setting). Calls one instance runs at the same time share its processor and Time per Call counts the time that passes, so a function that keeps the processor busy needs a Time per Call that covers Parallel Calls per Instance of them; see [Library calls time out during a burst](./troubleshooting.md#library-calls-time-out-during-a-burst).

Every instance that runs counts toward the app's limit on running Server Objects, and the Server Compute header shows it ("3 of 16 Server Objects"); past the limit no new instance starts. See [Large groups](./access-members-and-timers.md#large-groups) for the limits, and [Deploy it with Server Compute](./deploy-with-server-compute.md#overview) for how a library's row reads.

![The Server Compute row of loot_roller: its description, the line Server Function Library, 1 function and 1 instance, and the New badge](/img/unreal-sdk/server-compute-library.png)

With a Min Instances of 1 and a Max Instances of 4 the same line says "1 to 4 instances".

## Checks when you save

Saving a library lists these problems at once, with the usual checks of the visible settings (the Type Name, the function and server names, inputs and outputs). A library needs no State struct and no variable, so "Choose a State struct" does not apply to it.

- A function's **Callable By** is **Members** or **Leader**: "RollLoot: Members and Leader need members, and a Server Function Library has none."
- A function has a **Cooldown**: "RollLoot: a cooldown is saved data, and a Server Function Library saves none." Clear it. A library cannot remember when a player last called.
- **Max Instances** is below **Min Instances**: "Max Instances must be at least Min Instances."
- Another Server Object type lists the library under **Listen To**: "loot_roller is a Server Function Library and sends no messages." Remove it from **Listen To**; a type can still list it under **Can Call** and call it.

The editor shows the first two as a warning under the function's row too.

## Switch Kind

You can change **Kind** on a type that is already deployed, in either direction, and nothing is lost:

- **To Server Function Library.** The type's variables, members, timers, messages and the rest stay in the asset, hidden and unused. The server keeps the saved data of its Server Objects, unused, and it comes back if the type becomes a Server Object again. Server Objects of the type that were running stop answering. The deploy asks first and names the change: "loot_roller changes from Server Object to Server Function Library: its saved data is kept, unused, and comes back if it becomes a Server Object again."
- **To Server Object.** The hidden settings apply again, and the server starts each Server Object from the saved data it had, if any: "loot_roller changes from Server Function Library to Server Object: it starts from the saved data it had as a Server Object, if any."

Games that used the type change with it. A library has only **Call** nodes, and they take no **Target** and no Instance Id; **Get**, **On Changed** and **On Received** nodes of the type no longer apply, so remove them. See [Call a Server Function Library](./from-blueprint/call-a-library.md). The Server Compute row reads **Changed** with "kind" in its list, and **Changed** with "scaling" when only Min Instances, Max Instances or Parallel Calls per Instance differ from the live version.

After you change **Kind**, **Scaling** or a function, [generate the server code](./write-server-logic.md#after-you-change-the-definition) and deploy again.

## Write and call it

- **Its server code** is a `logic.rs` like a Server Object's, without a state: [A Server Function Library](./write-server-logic.md#a-server-function-library) shows the function, a library calling a Server Object, a Server Object calling a library, and what a library cannot do.
- **A game calls it** with the **Call RollLoot** node, or `UCrowdyServerObjectLibrary::CallLibraryFunction` in C++: [Call a Server Function Library, from Blueprint](./from-blueprint/call-a-library.md) and [from C++](./from-cpp/call-a-library.md).
- **Server code calls it** with a typed helper that takes no key, once the library is in the caller's **Can Call** list: [Call other Server Objects](./access-members-and-timers.md#call-other-server-objects).
